Every person, garment, object, and architectural plane exists only as ink contour and pigment on silk; adult figures use individualized mature facial lines, plausible adult proportions, clear joints, and exactly three bounded skin tones in the same silk plane. Flat gongbi picture plane with shallow stacked spaces, contour-enclosed color fields, dominant flat silhouettes, no volumetric light-and-shadow modeling or cast shadows. Fine gossamer-line and iron-wire contours, thin layered mineral-color washes, ruled-line jiehua interior, visible silk weave, softened outer pigments, rubbed silk fibers at one isolated edge, and small age creases. Center middle ground: the female calligrapher leans forward from the same couch, torso pitched toward image-left, pelvis bearing weight on right hip against the master carver's thigh, left hand spread flat on his chest beneath loosened robe collar, right hand cupping the inkstone's far edge; her head tilted to share his downward gaze, lips parted in warm scrutiny. Image-right middle ground: the male polishing companion kneels on one cushion at couch level, torso facing image-left, pelvis stabilized by grounded right knee, left knee raised; both hands extended forward, one holding a fine polishing brush, the other hovering to receive the stone, gaze lifted to meet the woman's downward-turned eyes with alert reciprocity. The current erotic but non-explicit interaction is the female calligrapher's left hand pressing inside the master carver's opened moon-white under-robe to spread palm flat against his bare chest, her outer cinnabar silk jacket fallen from one shoulder to expose the pale inner lining and her collarbones, his belt sash loosened to let trousers sag at hips, while the polishing companion watches with parted lips and deliberate forward lean, all three adults' gazes interlocked in triangular mutual acknowledgment.
